# Fidelity ledger

The goal is a one-to-one recreation of the offline Bubble Tanks 3 experience. **That goal is not yet complete.** Tests protect specific recovered contracts; they do not establish that an entire playthrough or every screen is indistinguishable from Flash.

## Source priority

1. The decompiled ActionScript in `../documented-bubble-tank-3/re/bt3/export_scripts_deobf/scripts/`.
2. Verified research documents, especially `research/02-core-gameplay.md` through `05-enemies-waves-difficulty.md`.
3. The Ruffle captures in `research/screenshots/` and `research/08-visual-reference.md`.
4. Earlier exported tables and dossier summaries when they do not conflict with the source.

The imported catalog contains 93 parts, 31 avatar templates, 43 enemies, one boss, two separately recovered ally designs, and 99 wave options. Wave rules, distance conditions, roster batches, mounted geometry, and original encrypted codes are retained.

## Implemented contracts

| System | Implemented behavior / evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Stage and clock | 550 × 450, default 50 FPS, optional 35/65 and 90 FPS efficiency mode; fixed original-frame integration; shared Main-effect clock through pause and menus |
| Movement | 0.6 acceleration, 6 × class speed axis clamp, 0.09 friction, 0.5 snap; unnormalized diagonal movement |
| Aim | Classes 1–3 turn hull to mouse; heavier hulls turn with movement and guns track mouse independently |
| Combat | Independent primary counters, shared secondary timer, original rates/damage/spreads and emitter positions; independent infection and forcefield recharge counters; stunned-state suspension of status clocks; shields |
| Damage / body loss | Integer damage, UI-only class armor, shield/forcefield ordering and recursive overflow; intact player bodies, incremental enemy/ally body degradation with original mount-order/outermost selection, retained healing debt; player hurt cooldown advances only with active tank frames |
| Collision | Current and half-step projectile points query retained geometric fill/stroke paths, nested masks and attached native effects; hidden/alpha-zero parts retain contact and detached parts do not. Thin strokes use the inverse display-tree transform, including game zoom; exact boundary/twip and broader runtime parity remain open below. Seeker interception uses transformed Script AABBs, including empty authored frames |
| Fields | Circular boundaries, nearest cardinal exit, 0.09 transit approach using the authored exterior-mask spacing, 317px entry markers × scale, saved living-enemy state, independent active field collections during crossings, retained destination shots/pickups/allies on arrival, revisitable cleared fields; only Apocalypse-destroyed holes are skipped; attached map crossings are recorded once; fresh attachment completion latches count empty revisits during transit |
| Waves | All 20/34/44 regular source options and the deadhead wave; strict local RULE filtering; initial and distance/traversal roster batches |
| Pickups | Recovered 1/5/50 drop distribution and large-drop remainder quirk, initial scatter with friction, fixed 100px attraction acquisition, class-scaled pull speed, collision before movement, next-frame collection latch, heal/XP coupling, forced collection on completion and field-owned collection sound timer |
| Progression | Exact life curve and post-upgrade/death health; upgrade choices; stored next-level designs; level-zero death escape |
| Advanced parts | Ally limits/designs, carrier detachment, mine cap, forcefield absorption/rearm, 26-frame Massive Cannon charge, two-contact damage beams, eleven-contact healer beams, contact-before-fuse Starburst, velocity-added Spinner helpers, pixel-gated Repulsor, per-mount Sapper shuttles, four-stage shield-turret building and builder interception, fresh Converter replacements; orbital steering and per-mount slots; Apocalypse wall fuse and 120-frame collapse |
| Boss | Current-health gate, once-per-session trigger, built-in boss, win overlay and local Boss Badge |
| Minimap | Native compact/large/hidden frame cycle, original grid scale and registration, uncleared/cleared/destroyed dot states, clipped traversal history, fixed current-position point; cycling keeps gameplay running |
| Field readouts | Native RadarArrow116, class-width enemy health bars1999, and100-frame ally health rings2194; fixed HUD dimensions, distinct interface/passive clocks, jam removal and stun suspension |
| Health / weapon HUD | Native life and nine weapon-panel layouts; first-mount primary grouping and short labels, primary/secondary fills, source 75-frame idle clocks and per-frame easing; mock geometry follows the same clock |
| Field guides / editing | Stopped native field-caption artwork, source pre-increment visit sequencing and revisit replacement, field-local movement; three native edit-tab variants with recovered entrance/retraction, confirmation and same-level replacement choices; replacement health restarts at the upgrade point; once-per-profile secondary help on replacement, immediate persistence and paused simulation |
| Tank information / win | Authored tank-info panels, class speed/armor, per-category sorted counts and abbreviations, current frozen mounted-part pose, sequential entrance and panel retraction; source win static copy, two retained controls, 40-frame preparation and authored fade, with retired pack promotion omitted |
| Session statistics / medals | Exact roster-code classification and removal-time medal polling, David and Goliath, source equality thresholds, pickup-object count, separate friendly/enemy shot and virtual-boundary miss counts, nominal direct-contact damage accounting; creator saves new IDs and checks three retained custom designs |
| Local tools | Tank creator with vertical palette, drag placement, additive click/marquee group selection, mirrored group movement, group scale/rotation/layer order/delete/undo, faded twin selection, authored class rings, alpha hit selection, class pixel counting, budgets/unlocks, saved library, JSON and original tank codes |
| Presentation | Supplied SWF vectors/fonts, original menu art, per-frame native tank/projectile/pickup timelines, live creator previews/drafts/placed parts, native masks and dynamically stretched beam children, four authored turret construction states, attached sticky/jam/stun/weaken clips, infection overlay/color matrix, authored ink screen animation, shield and forcefield clips/impact frames, original extracted music/SFX and frame-based music fades; Main-level shake and independent flashes; procedural mock fallback |

## Remaining work before claiming one-to-one parity

- Finish screen transitions and exact creator typography/selection controls. Tank-info foreground geometry was visually compared with original capture40; its native static labels and embedded Arial dynamic fields now replace the generic stats card. The pause-orb/Info crossfade, controlled comparison of the recovered live clip clocks during pause, general pause/settings/upgrade/creator tween timing, and complete side-by-side pixel comparison remain. Win foreground geometry/fade follows source, but its runtime appearance has not been captured. Pause now uses a retained bitmap and source-ordered blur history; exact GPU rounding and complete stage capture coverage still need comparison. Other screen blurs remain CSS approximations.
- Finish HUD side-by-side runtime comparison. The base movie has no lock-on registration caller, so Q has no target effect; future parts-pack hooks are retained. Secondary help now follows the source replacement trigger, immediate persistent latch and explicit Close behavior, with foreground geometry visually checked against original capture20. Health/weapon widgets and field guides now use the authored artwork; edit-slider variants and distance-scaled motion follow the source. Device-font metrics, complete HUD/gameplay update ordering, unusual designs exceeding the nine authored primary rows, and a controlled original recording of edit-tab motion still need comparison.
- Verify every advanced part against a controlled original run. In particular: compare the newly recovered Apocalypse fuse/collapse, orbital steering and three-point shot-down masks, and rocket weave/trails against Flash. Healer contact/range, the shared primary-beam sample grid, turret construction, attached status/ink artwork, and shield/forcefield clips now follow recovered source and have deterministic native-art fixtures, but still need side-by-side Flash recordings. Protection sizing now follows the recovered ordered Script-bound algorithm and NaN fallback, with controlled original-runtime comparison still pending. Remaining projectile callbacks, infection target-selection quirks, and collision against attached status artwork also need verification.
- Compare the recovered frame-script playback against controlled original recordings, particularly nested clip identity and timing through jumps. Random starts, Y branches, cluster loops, stop frames, and independently advancing children are now implemented for all 25 scripted clips reachable from the part catalog. Non-part callbacks that perform drawing, effects, sound, or unrelated application work remain outside the supported frame-action set. All 55 morph definitions now interpolate geometry and styles at authored placement ratios, including nested collision masks; compare their playback timing and appearance directly against Flash.
- Compare sprite output with FFDec/running Flash across the full catalog, including winding joins, gradient/bitmap sampling, masks and anti-aliasing. Alpha tests currently use rasterized vector leaves at 2× resolution.
- Validate complete progression through the boss with normal player input and match the original boss/win timing. Unit tests cover the gate and transitions, not a full-playthrough parity recording.
- Compare all local medal conditions and audio against the original. Empty-field revisits now use a fresh `_‑FR` equivalent and count during the first transit frame, including revisiting home; boss/win choreography and medal edge cases still need runtime comparison. Music uses the shared stage for its40-frame fade-in/out and20-frame post-change fade-in; SFX retain the source ten general slots. MP3 seek/count windows, accepted transition requests, mute memory and collision-owned hit cooldowns are recovered below. Source shock/charging dispatch, complete route/loop notifications and synchronized timing still require verification. The reserved interface slots have no discovered base-movie SoundCall caller.
- Compare damage/degradation with a controlled original combat recording, especially the retained debt/minimum during enemy healing and the distinct ally behavior. The player branch and empty `AvatarLifeCorrect` were confirmed in bytecode; the old universal health-fraction body reconstruction was incorrect. Projectile hit effects/sounds and lethal-player transition ordering still need comparison across the full world update loop.
- Compare crossing runtime activity with a controlled original recording. Both field populations now run during state2, using independent collections and local coordinates; existing shots/pickups continue, new shots are gated, arrival retains destination collections, and enemy removal/completion uses the following field frame. The live bullet loop processes appended fragments immediately. Destination enemy boundaries, death drops and pickup bounds now retain the original outgoing `currentBFTarget` behavior. Remaining differences include other targeting/effect callbacks that consult that wrapper, other cross-field targeting/effect callbacks, remaining attached-object identity and event-order quirks beyond the new tank/protection stage clocks, and exact asynchronous Timer/event-loop ordering under stalls.
- Compare creator center-line snap offsets and twin merge/respawn against a controlled original run; finish exact selection-bound rendering, remaining input/event ordering and controlled original save/code transitions. The source onboarding, retained alert, save defaults and accepted-part validation/history are recovered below; full comparison remains open. Compare the newly animated previews/placed parts with the original across the catalog, including constructor timing and nested clip lifetimes through jumps. Palette selection and preview dragging now follow the separate source actions, with repeated drops, empty enemy drafts (stat-default differences below), unpadded authored radii, three-pixel placement snap, and preserved selection. Click toggling, additive marquee radius samples, mirrored group movement, faded twin selection, group scale/rotation/delete/ordering, partial-pair unlinking, symmetry-toggle clearing, and undo have source-backed regressions and mock/native browser coverage. The incremental drag controller covers axis merging/respawn and the bytecode's unusual half-pixel/positive-delta snap behavior; axis input/selection state still needs controlled comparison. The palette now follows the source cost/class checks, unlock/cost/ID ordering, first affordable default, and dimmed but selectable unaffordable rows. Placement uses the recovered rings [35,55,75,100,137.5,175], large-class rectangular bounds, and body/weapon distinction. Render quality uses raster resolution/sampling rather than Flash StageQuality internals.

## Deliberate removals and stubs

Retired community queries, sharing/ratings, online accounts, parts-pack purchase/download, advertising/analytics, and external portal medals/stat calls do not run. Their adapters are isolated in `src/game/services/extensions.ts`. The menu uses the local library in place of unavailable online tools; obsolete Flash permission tutorials, sponsor redirects, and dead website links are omitted.

Artwork is not downloaded or bundled. Mock mode uses procedural paint, with recovered body/weapon/module timelines and projectile dimensions. Its simplified contours, highlights and animation remain distinct from the supplied original artwork.

Requested usability additions include Escape as a pause key, pausing during a crossing, persistent camera zoom from 50–150% (100% preserves the original view), player firing sounds, and synthesized mock-mode audio. The Internet Archive download guide asks the player to obtain and select their own SWF; it does not download the file automatically. Music playback retries inside user gestures after browser media suspension without replacing an otherwise-current track.

The map cycle button also accepts Enter/Space and has a screen-reader description of map position and path count.

Keyboard preview placement is an accessibility addition: activate the preview, move with arrows, and confirm/cancel with Enter/Escape. Pointer placement follows the original preview-drag flow.

The former allied-Healer valid-index workaround has been removed. The shipped inclusive one-based lookup, final self/healthy candidate and missing-entry read now follow the recovered source; see the targeting recovery entry below. The known missing-entry error aborts the current World/Main.Run listener while later stage frames continue. This error-boundary mapping is supported by pinned reference-runtime source, rather than an Adobe execution comparison.

## Verification

- `bun run check`: TypeScript plus deterministic world, progression, rules, creator validation, parser, and tank-code regressions.
- `bun run test:e2e`: production build plus mock and supplied-SWF browser paths, actual media playback/resumption and player shot audio, local creator/library/settings, cleared-field return travel and deduplicated map paths, P/Escape pause, fullscreen/zoom, a saved Class 6 avatar firing and live quality changes, invalid input, and small viewport sizing.
- Optional reference tests check all 1,071 shape/morph definitions, all 55 morphs at five interpolation ratios, embedded font decoding, corrected matrices/alpha, and all 75 original tank codes when the research repository is present.
- `bun run fixtures` opens a development-only deterministic viewer on port 3102. Supply the local SWF to inspect healing and damage beams, all four turret stages, status/ink/protection artwork, a downgrade and restored secondary, crossing frames, and health/weapon HUD layouts; browser regressions check beam collision, effect placement counts, infection health changes, restored weapon state, camera scale, travel completion, and HUD fill pixels. This tool does not add fixture routes to the game.
- The morph fixture compares synchronous Canvas rendering with independent browser SVG rasterization for six representative morphs at start/middle/end ratios (18 comparisons), checks a nested projectile's moving alpha mask, and checks the raster cache's pixel bound. These checks do not establish pixel identity with Flash.
- Creator browser tests import a known design, select and move a mirrored group, marquee-select it again, scale/rotate/reorder/delete/undo, then move one part in asymmetric mode. They also merge a pair on the axis, move it away to respawn a linked twin, and undo that change. They inspect exported design geometry and twin links in both mock and supplied-SWF modes.
- Placement browser tests start with an empty enemy at life/reward/speed 1, verify that palette selection alone adds nothing, then drop repeated previews with/without symmetry. They check reciprocal twins, 12.6-pixel Medium Bubble radii, center snapping, retained selection, outside-drop/cancel behavior, keyboard placement, and undo through exported geometry in both asset modes. Live Ruffle preview drags also showed separate palette selection, mirrored placement, and a new body snapping onto the axis when released within three pixels of it.
- `BT3_REFERENCE=1` enables the optional original-SWF browser smoke test. It loads a local file into the development-only Ruffle 0.7.1 viewer, waits for the original Continue artwork, clicks it with native input, and verifies the storage tutorial's dark panel. The owned reference browser uses a 4096 KB V8 stack to avoid the observed WASM stack overflow. REA capture `ev_8d62b9995c7acb3f9b67de91aa9209be5bd4ab434a01966ae68722757a9dfce1` confirmed successful original preloader startup; native Playwright coordinate input was then used for the menu/creator route because REA scenario clicks do not support canvas coordinates. Ruffle emulation is not proof of Adobe Flash parity.
- Screen browser checks open and close tank information twice from actual gameplay in both asset modes, inspect its class/weapon fields and settled portrait origin, and return through pause. A development-only fixture mounts the production win component in both modes and checks completed frame timing, button callbacks, and omission of the retired promotion. It does not replace a full boss playthrough.
- The damage fixture renders the same native/mock mounted geometry at full enemy health, after damage, after enemy healing, and as a damaged player. Pixel hashes verify visual body loss/restoration and the complete player body. The current alpha-contact assertions also change with visibility, but the collision audit below identifies that behavior as a source mismatch. Unit traces separately protect the source's accumulated debt, mount ordering, healing distinctions, integer arguments, armor display-only behavior, and active-frame hurt timer.

- The active-crossing fixture renders four actual simulation/renderer snapshots in mock and native modes. Browser checks verify actor and native shot positions from both fields, destination collection retention, and outgoing collection disposal. Unit tests cover local avatar collision, pickup collection during transit, pause, rejected shots with advancing enemy counters, next-frame completion, boundary initiation and the frozen later avatar phase. These fixtures establish reconstruction behavior; they are not original-runtime pixel comparisons.

Keep this ledger current. Move a remaining item into verified coverage only after inspecting the original behavior and recording the corresponding regression or visual evidence.

## Recent source corrections

- `AvatarInterface._‑1r` triggers mounted primaries before field combat. `Weapon.Run` later aims/decrements their counters in Avatar.Run; large classes consequently fire using the previous part aim. HUD health/weapon fills are snapshots from `_‑MO`, before field combat and cooldown updates. `_‑Ta` advances passives before infection/weaken maintenance. Secondary readiness decrements to0, then resets to-1 on a separate interface frame; this interface clock continues during crossings and freezes in pause.
- Player Rocket Launcher3015 (`_‑VG`) uses `Timer(100,10)`: first shot100ms after activation, then ten callbacks at100ms spacing, with emitters1–5 repeated and current muzzle/rotation sampled on each callback. The timer continues through jam/stun/pause. Ordinary crossing `_‑ZY → _‑1J` and avatar replacement cancel it; level-zero `_‑1E(true)` bypasses FinalPartCall. Rejected transit callbacks are consumed, not replayed after arrival. Enemy4020 remains part-clocked, retaining its six-call interval between bursts.
- Beam2017 has one maintained projectile with explicit stop events, not a two-frame expiry. Mouse release removes it even while world simulation is paused; normal crossing and replacement also stop it. Source input dispatch and tank-part Run are separate. The browser clock fixture mounts the production viewport and tests held-beam pause/release plus the continuing launcher timer at35/90FPS with both asset modes.
- Massive Cannon/shield callbacks follow authored26/25-frame timelines. Stage frames continue while pause omits effects-engine polling, so a completed frame can loop before resumed polling observes it. Polling happens after field bullets; callback-born shots wait for the next field update. Arrival `_‑4j → _‑8Y` drops all registered effect timelines/text and callbacks from both fields without firing callbacks, then promotes destination bullets/pickups/allies. The previous destination-effect retention assumption was incorrect. Generic visual effects and remaining part timelines still require independent-clock reconciliation.
- Auto Cannon5003 and Seeker Flak5004 fire at their retained target, then acquire the next target. Their first eligible call acquires without firing; equal-distance acquisition chooses the last candidate. Auto aiming uses the nearest mounted detail and rounded flight prediction. Flak's source seeker scan does not filter by enemy ownership. Orbital slots do not reset their cooldown when already full. Firing sound uses the owning field's5→0→-1 counter and only friendly projectile insertion; pause freezes that counter while wall-timed rockets can still be created. Browser-clock firing throttling was removed, matching the earlier collection/hurt separation.

- `_‑Pu.Run` checks bounds, damps initial scatter before moving, and begins attraction only after scatter speed falls below0.5 (or completion cancels scatter). Acquisition is strictly within100px, independent of class; class cubed affects attraction speed. Field `LifeBubbleCollisionDetection` runs first and collects by alpha contact or the preceding movement's `blnCollected` latch. The sub10px latch therefore grants health on the following field frame. Forced attraction is14px/frame. Collection audio uses a field-owned4→0→-1 counter; there is no browser-clock collection throttle.
- `SpawnLifeBubbles` replaces the last five with five singles when a sub50 reward has more than one five and a nonzero remainder. For rewards of at least50, its one-bubble count incorrectly uses the number of fives modulo5; pcode4011–4014 confirms this is shipped behavior, not a decompiler artifact. The recreation preserves that distribution (e.g.51 drops50;55 drops56), rather than silently substituting greedy denominations. Constructor scatter speed is rolled before rotation, and native pickup views retain rotation.
- Non-player active Run is hit timer → movement/sticky → jam → ordered parts → decoration/protection maintenance → ink/infection/weaken → boundary. Infection can kill a tank after its last shot. Stunned Run advances hit/stun/maintenance only, including its entire reset frame. Enemy boundaries exempt bosses and use Damage; ally boundaries deactivate directly despite shields/forcefields. Enemy and ally loops are separate live arrays, so newly spawned enemies run immediately. Native-art browser fixtures protect pickup contact/visibility/health phase alignment; unit tests protect actor order, reset frames, live spawn insertion and outgoing-wrapper boundary/drop behavior.
- Symbols701 (`_‑KE`, part4015) and25 (`_‑IB`, part4017) use per-part counters that freeze when Run is suppressed. The rotating gun retains mount angle and alternates100 decrements plus a separate switch frame; its ordinary firing counter pauses during its inactive phase. Suppression uses a rate1 trigger, a120-call delay, four skipped triggers between shots, and the original below-50 reset. These replace the previous tank-age modulo shortcuts. Source-backed tests establish these cadences; controlled original combat recordings are still required.

- `BTAGameWorld.Run` state2 runs interface → current field → destination field → effects → crossing movement, without `myAvatar.Run`. Boundary detection starts within state0 before its ordinary calls, so the initiation frame still runs the avatar once. `_‑0D.SpawnNewBullet` gates insertion on `_‑5d`; it does not freeze existing shots or weapon timers.
- `BTAGameWorld._‑4j` detaches the outgoing field/layers, caches only surviving enemies and promotes the destination's live layers. `_‑0D.Deactivate` cleans up only if `!_‑Ol && _‑FR` (confirmed in pcode); unfinished outgoing collections become detached rather than being transferred or explicitly killed. Reattachment restores enemy objects into a fresh wrapper with new collections. Arrival calls `AvatarInterface._‑4d` to zero movement, clears the player shield, and resets player primary counters, leaving destination enemy counters intact.

- `AvatarInterface.Run` performs input/shooting and HUD motion before `_‑dI`, then `BTAGameWorld.Run` calls the field and avatar in that order. Ordinary full-health upgrades and higher-level deaths are checked on the next interface frame, after shots/pickups from the previous field update. A higher-level lethal hit can therefore be healed by a pickup before the downgrade check. `_‑Zx.Damage` handles level-zero death separately and starts `_‑1E(true)` synchronously, resetting life to1. `_‑Ud` resolves center/diagonal ties in left→right→top→bottom order with strict comparisons; center escapes left, not down. The recreation keeps a single active crossing when repeated lethal impacts arrive during the escape.
- Player infection and passive weapons now run after the field update. New primary shots take their first field step immediately; new passive shots wait for the next field step. Massive Cannon completion now polls the authored26-frame timeline after field bullets; the new projectile has age0 until the following field update. Its timeline keeps looping while pause suspends engine polling. Upgrade choice and secondary-help dialogs remember an interrupted crossing and restore it on close instead of abandoning it in playing mode. This dialog return behavior also preserves the requested pause-during-crossing addition; remaining full-loop differences are listed above.
- `AttachNewBubblefield` consults cached destinations before its death-escape wave flag. A level-zero escape generates deadheads only for an unvisited destination; an existing field, including cleared home, is preserved. The avatar's stunned branch skips part clocks through its reset frame, while AvatarInterface direction/movement/manual trigger dispatch remains separate. The prior global control-freeze assumption was incorrect. In the shipped base source, the only StunMe caller targets the enemy array; forced-player-stun tests exercise the underlying separation without implying a naturally occurring player stun weapon.

- `_‑Zx.Damage` calls `DegradeTank` only for surviving non-player tanks. Its player branch plays the gated hurt sound; `AvatarLifeCorrect` has an empty bytecode body (pcode3739–3761). Player bodies therefore remain intact at low health and on replacement. Degradation's total divides by all mounted parts, and `_‑GX` picks the outermost visible body using distance plus stored radius; `DegradeTank` falls back through mount order, never a sorted “first weight at least debt” selection. Debt and the cached visible minimum survive between calls. Enemy `AddLife` restores body visibility then adds missing-health debt again; ally `AddLife` does not restore bodies, and only avatar designs cap healed life.
- Damage parameters coerce to signed integer before weaken. Forcefield overflow recursively calls Damage, so an artificially combined weaken/forcefield state doubles overflow again; the shipped forcefield blocks acquiring weaken, making this an extension-contract case. `_‑9E` gates player hurt sounds for seven decrements plus the zero-to-minus-one frame; pause/transit suspend that tank clock. Infection's `_‑bN` runs before `_‑J1` clears weaken on its final active frame. Class armor remains UI-only, as in the source.

- `PauseMenu._-Bz` groups categories2/3/5 by type, sorts IDs, and switches each category to short names only when it has more than two distinct types. Single full names have no count prefix; plural labels use the imported `strPluralName`. `_‑cG` displays class speed and armor rather than current life or creator budgets, and reparents the existing avatar. The shared `TankView` paints the same mounted rotations, hidden/removed parts, status, and protection state for gameplay and the frozen Info portrait. `_‑HP` and `_‑U0` supply the panel motion; pause-orb choreography remains an approximation.
- `_‑EG`/symbol2276 has no statistics or dynamic TextFields. Its 39 preparation frames perform19 blur passes; frame40 starts2275 atframe2, fading through24 and stopping at25. Continue restores play; Main Menu leaves the run. Numerical placements and alpha values come from `tools/import-win-geometry.ts`; the original pack-manager control and promotional copy are omitted through that importer.

- `_-14/_-6.as` freezes Apocalypse at the visual bubble wall while its fuse runs; it does not immediately kill the field. `_-MC` (2173) ends after 120 frames; `_-0D.ShrinkBubblefield` subtracts 1/204 scale each frame. The implementation marks the field destroyed and escapes at the end. Reward/transition choreography still needs a runtime comparison.
- `_-14/_-JX.as` retains its orbital projectile kind while pursuing a target, steers back by signed angle/20 outside the ring, and turns 5 degrees within it. `_-9U` and `_-Vc` pass radians + 90 to the degree-based spawn API; that quirk is retained. Opposing shots survive shooting down these seekers.
- `_-14/_-Kk.as` gives rockets a randomized 0.10–0.20 sine frequency and 0.1–0.8 radian amplitude with counter starting at 45. `_-14/_-dO.as` jitters movement around its original heading rather than accumulating rotation.
- Projectile symbol/scale mappings are imported from the actual `SpawnNewBullet` call sites into `projectile-art.json`. Sapper/mine state frames are selected explicitly rather than playing through unrelated timeline states.
- `_-14/HealerBeam.as` and its pcode advance healing after eleven pixel-contact frames and preserve the counter across interruptions. The unusual `sqrt(dx * dx + dy + dy)` integer prefilter is retained. `_-ct/EBehaviorHealer.as` owns a patient independently of offensive targeting, updates its beam every three frames, and stops it beyond 400 pixels.
- `_-14/_-CO.as` damages after two contact frames, retaining partial contact across interruptions. Every target uses the same six-pixel sampling grid starting at the truncated minimum center-distance minus radius; the signed sine broad-phase quirk is retained. Beam children use the source's line width, mask width plus four, and endpoint position.
- `_-ct/_-DO.as`, `EnemyTurret.as`, and `EnemyTurretBuilder.as` build four stages at the weapon registration point, use straight builders, retain completed turrets while starting another, and preserve opposing shots that intercept builders. A completed turret shields its owner; its stopped inner view turns toward the owner.
- SWF `StateNewStyles` appends a new indexed style table and starts a new paint layer. Shape 683 demonstrated the previous error: merging old edges into new colors canceled the center under even-odd filling. The parser now keeps style offsets/layer order, reverses fill-left edges, joins directed boundaries, and omits internal edges shared by the same fill.
- `_-Zx.Run` pauses jam, ink, weaken, infection, and enemy movement's sticky timer while stunned, while attached effect clips and forcefield recharge continue. Infection's counter starts when infected and damages every twelve active frames; forcefield replenishment counts twenty frames with damage debt. Neither counter is tied to tank age.
- `_-Zx.EffectMeCrazy` replaces earlier marks, places one per part below five parts, and otherwise picks five parts with replacement. Sticky/jam/weaken hold a randomized variant while nested clips keep playing; stun plays from a randomized starting frame. `_-U4` supplies the infection overlay, random rotation, and color matrix. `_-ar` applies ink only to the avatar without refreshing an existing timer; a forcefield suppresses its visual effect while retaining its timer. The ink clip has its own authored 100-frame lifetime.
- `BTAGameWorld.AttachNewBubblefield` skips only stored `"destroyed"` fields, not ordinary cleared fields. Its transit layout uses the exterior mask's authored 1391.9px width, not the visible bubble's 847.5px diameter; geometry is imported by `tools/import-swf-geometry.ts`.
- `AGunShield` uses the 25-frame `_-YE` effect as its lifetime, with `_-BF` display art and the directional `_-Ne` impact. The turret shield uses `_-bM` and `_-KJ`. `APassiveForcefield` clips its splash to a cloned full design; `forcefieldanimation_551` stops at frame 1, returns there at 25, loops broken frames 26–34, and rearms from 36.
- Morph edge records contain empty style arrays; spreading those records after the tag's styles erased every morph's fills/strokes. The tag parser now preserves those styles, end gradient offsets/focal values, and signed FIXED8 fields. Interpolation pairs edges across differing run boundaries and converts straight/curved pairs to quadratics. Gradient matrix coefficients retain eight decimal places.
- `TankCreatorScreen._-L3` toggles individual selected parts without requiring Shift; `SelectObject` also selects and fades a linked twin. `_‑bp` adds parts whose center/cardinal radius samples intersect the marquee, retaining its duplicated positive half-Y sample. `StartDrag` records which side of the initial pointer each selected part occupies and `_‑5M` mirrors the movement on the other side. `_‑CI` sets every selected scale absolutely; `_‑aJ` sets absolute rotations with sign determined by Y. `DeleteObject` removes only that object and breaks its surviving twin's link; `_‑6z` unlinks partially selected pairs after editing. `SymmetricModeMouseUp` clears selection. The recreation keeps this transient selection state outside serialized designs.
- `StartDrag` samples its grab offset on the first move, while preserving the pointer-down side for every selected part. `_-5M` examines pre-move positions, considers the nearest selected part within 0.5 pixels (inside a redundant 3-pixel test), and retains the positive entry delta shown in both ActionScript and AVM2 bytecode. Exact-axis merging removes the faded member of a selected pair, even when it is the pointer-side reference. `RespawnTwinObject` inserts a new reciprocal twin immediately after its owner, copies scale/type/radius, mirrors Y/rotation, fades it, and gives it the opposite drag side. The controller normalizes only browser coordinate roundoff within 0.00001 pixels of zero.
- Live original menu clicks corrected the visual-reference notes: the left plus is Parts Packs, and the right bubble cluster is Enemy Tank Creator. `_‑Sm.mmBtnUp` confirms the handlers; sprite 556 places symbol 496 as `partsPacks_btn` at (145.85,228.7), and symbol 500 as `enemyCreator_btn` at (365.85,228.7). The recreation now places the creator on the right and the deliberate local-library replacement on the left, with matching mock icon shapes.
- `TankCreatorScreen._-3N` initially assigns life/reward/speed1/1/1, but its later enemy constructor call `_‑13:4642–4653` replaces these with20/20/2 and enables suggested settings. The recreation now uses the later20/20/2 defaults, retained suggestion flag, phase-specific save update and authored enemy panel described below; complete constructor/frame ordering comparison remains open. `_‑Jo` changes the selected palette item and preview; `StartLibraryDrag` begins placement from the preview. `_‑8a` snaps new objects at |Y| ≤ 3 even in asymmetric mode and fades a mirrored draft to 25%. `_‑ZW` commits valid members individually, restores their opacity, and does not call `SelectObject`. The selected palette item and any earlier placed-part selection remain available after the drop. Its stored radius is the newly constructed clip's width/2, so raster padding must be excluded.

- `TankCreatorScreen.UpdateAffordableArray` checks one object against complexity, remaining Gun Points, and inclusive class limits independently of symmetry and locks. `_‑ay` sorts by lock state, numeric Gun Point cost, then object ID; `_‑1X` chooses the first affordable unlocked row. `UpdateLibraryButtonsForAffordability` only changes opacity to 0.4, so unaffordable unlocked rows remain selectable and placement is allowed; save validation enforces the final build. Body rows omit cost indicators and free weapons display 0. Mock/SWF browser regressions cover palette ordering, dimmed selection, empty default preview, budget/complexity overflow, and undo.

- Creator `_‑Jo` constructs a new live preview clip; `_‑Zl` constructs separate live drag clips and `_‑ZW` retains them after placement. `UpdateClass` builds a fresh body-only clip through `TankData._‑Tr(4)` for its pixel snapshot. The editor now reuses the native display-list renderer for preview, draft, and placed artwork, keeps per-part ages through edits/drops, and uses matching animated alpha tests. Two original Ruffle captures showed 985 changed preview pixels and 232 changed placed-part pixels in their respective crops. This confirms animation exists; it does not prove synchronized frame identity or complete MovieClip script semantics.

- `tools/import-timeline-scripts.ts` recovers 230 complete frame-action tables and checks the original SWF's part subtree plus the three mine roots: all 25 scripted part clips and the mine roots are covered. It rejects an unrecovered reachable script rather than partially importing its class. Eighteen other classes contain unsupported callbacks, including application/retired-service code and several projectile effects. It recorded 3,697 solid-interior pixel samples with zero misses and zero spurious hits against WebGL output; partial-alpha pixels and antialiasing boundaries are excluded. ### Field completion and secondary help

- `_‑0D.TestForCompletion` (1543–1574) suppresses only the initial `blnFirstBubblefield` wrapper and its own `_‑FR` latch. `AttachNewBubblefield` reconstructs both flags; cached empty enemy lists do not preserve completion. `core/field-visit.ts` models that live wrapper separately from persistent `Field.cleared`. Pausing freezes it. Destroyed fields do not complete. Completed counts and unique minimap paths remain independent.

Masochist compares the maximum absolute coordinate against the unique-field counter. Exact boss/win transition behavior still needs comparison.

### Combat effect callbacks and native timelines

- The source effect engine (`_-2c.CreateEffect`, `Run`, `_‑9p`, `_‑8Y`) separates registration, independent MovieClip playback, active-state completion polling and global removal at field arrival. `_‑0D.Run` flushes queued requests after combat/pickups/completion; rocket trails and Neutralizer effects register immediately. Both field wrappers share the outgoing registered layers during a crossing. The model now retains those queues, layer ownership and pause-loop/cleanup distinction for ordinary combat clips. Exact Flash stage/event interleaving and queues interrupted by Apocalypse still require original-runtime comparison.
- `tools/import-effect-art.ts` recovers 12 reachable roots and their lifetimes: impact1723/4, leech1740/18, damage_burst1745/16, explosion1746/15, seeker1767/10, mine1834/6, trail2175/25, area1789/25, teleport1769/12, conversion1735/21, Ripple52/15 and Neutralizer2212/29. Native lower/upper display-list playback preserves authored geometry, morphs and child alpha. No extracted artwork is bundled. Ordinary tank Damage/Deactivate have no generic hit/death ring, and area damage has no universal burst; those invented effects were removed. Shield and Massive Cannon keep their attached artwork/callback timers without extra generic rings.
- Projectile callbacks select their own effects. Ordinary impacts, turret death/owner loss/wall expiry and builder contact use1723; seekers/orbitals use1767; mines use1834; leech uses1740. Pierce2009 has no extra explosion despite area damage,2010 adds1746, Massive Cannon adds1746, and Area Burst Cannon adds1745. Maintained beams, push, infection contact and anti-seeker flak add no generic contact/burst. Whip preserves the source's global-point-to-local-queue quirk. The following projectile-order pass reconciles the ordinary phases, wall expiry, several shot-down callbacks, infection expiry and Leech pickups; unusual subclasses and runtime comparison remain. These changes do not establish every projectile's parity.
- Avatar/EnemyRocket use the inherited `_‑Kk` trail: after every third successful movement, register2175 and draw a line to the previous trail origin. The first trail is empty. Each segment is white, width2, alpha0.3; `line_mc`'s authored alpha falls from1 to0 over25 frames. Native and mock modes now use connected fading lines instead of smoke dots. Rocket weave/contact timing still needs a controlled original capture.
- Area stun/burst artwork and tests now use the emitter and exact145px center range. Teleport emits1769 at both endpoints, clamps to the full field radius, flashes, gates repeated teleport calls and follows `CheckForTeleports`' slot swaps. The source explicitly passes `(this.x,this.y)` to `this.localToGlobal`, applying the weapon registration offset twice; the model preserves that quirk. Ripple waits ready until it pushes a target, truncates the radius gap, replaces ally force, and emits once. Neutralizer starts ready at240, advances only during the current wrapper's Apocalypse fuse flag, and immediately registers a scale3 clip for each removed projectile. The source's live-array splice/index behavior is preserved. These advanced parts still need progressed original-runtime comparison.
- Eight source-contract regressions cover callback selection, absence of invented rings, paused playback/polling, shared crossing layers/arrival removal, connected rocket segments, teleport positions/order, burst radius, Ripple and Neutralizer clocks. The isolated browser fixture checks native/mock trail alpha at frames1/13/25, cleanup on resume, nonempty output for all12 roots, and upper/lower occlusion. Native trail samples agree with30%/15%/0 alpha. The gallery was visually inspected. These are deterministic reconstruction checks, not Adobe Flash pixel identity.

The full browser suite passed39 tests with the optional original-reference smoke skipped. The native gallery and live-flight screenshots were visually inspected. Preview port3100 was restarted with the new build. This does not close the remaining full-game fidelity gaps above.

### Projectile ordering, flags and special callbacks

- `_‑BL.MoveBullet` checks the415×field-scale wall, aims, tests current/backward-half-step points, then moves. Wall expiry happens on the following Run after crossing the boundary. `_‑bk` reflects and moves during its bounce frame, allowing six bounces. `_‑Un`/`_‑dp` and Grow ignore a positive contact result and continue moving. The simulation now follows those ordinary phases rather than moving before its collision scan. Rocket weave updates after its contact test. Base collision retains the source's early empty-enemy-array guard, including for hostile bullets, and the friendly current-point enemy-layer gate before per-target half-step tests. These unusual guards are source behavior.
- Massive Cannon tests the center, four cardinal offsets at half its MovieClip width, and the backward half-step. Its separate prior-hit loop skips old targets; ordinary/piercing hit memory returns early on an old target. Carrier interception uses the current point. Native width comes from authored bounds with projectile scale/rotation; the mock fallback uses its drawn diameter. Exact independent clip phase, filter/display-bound quirks and status-art collision remain comparison gaps.
- `AreaDamageTest` callbacks precede direct impact damage. Friendly splash excludes only the current direct target, not all earlier piercing hits, and uses the strict1.15×radius center test. Enemy area damage uses avatar center-distance minus avatar radius with an inclusive radius, separate ally-center checks, and the source's contact-object center for opposing-projectile cleanup. Only Destroyable projectiles are removed, without impact callbacks; dead enemies are flushed before returning from area damage. Complete live-array/event ordering during simultaneous removals still needs comparison.
- `tools/import-projectile-flags.ts` recovers33 constructor flag sets through inheritance. Seeker and Destroyable are distinct: enemy seekers/orbitals have both; sapper is Destroyable only; infection is Seeker only; avatar seeker has neither; seeker mine becomes Seeker only when armed. Anti-seeker acquisition follows Seeker, area cleanup follows Destroyable. The former kind-based lists incorrectly targeted sappers and avatar seekers. Default `blnBeam=true` is confirmed by `_‑BL` constructor/pcode, with no shipped false assignment; turret contact therefore preserves incoming shots in these classes.
- Opposite-shot interception now applies to enemy seeker/orbital classes rather than infection, sapper or avatar seeker. Enemy basic seekers test only the avatar after their shot scan; orbitals support allies. Armed mine contact invokes its own callback followed by the incoming callback without explicitly consuming the incoming shell. The following mine pass replaces its shared point samples with Script AABBs and reconciles its unarmed contact/scatter phases.
- Bubble Buster2012 (`_‑c4`) deflects the first nearby enemy projectile within11.5+half its width. It copies the buster movement into base Run state2; it does not delete the shot, invoke its impact callback or change its displayed rotation. State2 bypasses subtype homing/contact and moves before checking the wall. Native/mock simulation and rendered fixtures cover the retained movement state.
- Leech2011 (`_‑El`) queues1740 and spawns one forced value1 life bubble before direct damage, including shield-blocked contact. The pickup seeks `world.myAvatar`; it never directly heals the firing owner. Collection uses the ordinary following-frame latch/accounting. The simulation now follows that path. The generic volley constructor no longer gives shots an invented500-frame lifetime. The following special-projectile pass removes the remaining fragment/conversion and long orbital/turret timeouts; explicitly recovered finite subtype clocks remain separate.
- Infection3013's `_‑U4._‑26` reads Array.x/y and tests element0's infection flag. The pcode confirms the missing index; NaN prevents ordinary target acquisition. Both the weapon and infected-death spawns pass null, so Setup queues removal/nonrandom1767, and the first field Run can repeat that effect before removal. The previous nearest-enemy repair was removed. A supplied valid target still uses the original infection-before-damage callback, including through protection. A bounded loop guard prevents reproducing a potential hang when a changed enemy count falls below an old retry count. Exact duplicate-effect appearance and this shipped targeting limitation still need original-runtime comparison; the base bug is not silently repaired.
- Eleven new source-contract tests cover pre-movement/wall timing, layer and empty-array guards, Massive width samples, splash/direct order, flags, deflection, Leech collection, interception, infection failure and anti-seeker acquisition. The flak clock regression now supplies a valid retained infection target so it tests acquisition rather than the separate shipped acquisition failure.
- Verification for this pass:202 rule/parser/data tests with1880 assertions, typecheck, the final production build and formatting passed. The full browser suite passed41 tests with the optional original-reference smoke skipped. After the final Massive Cannon prior-hit/carrier guard, all four affected native/mock projectile-contact and production-clock flows passed again. The native three-frame contact screenshot was visually inspected; a copy is retained at `/tmp/bt3-native-projectile-order.png`. The preview on3100 was restarted with the final build. Ordinary phase/flag/callback contracts are improved; the full one-to-one goal and the listed mine/subclass/runtime gaps remain open.

### Mine phases, independent clocks and per-mount references

- `_‑XK.MoveBullet:23` now has a dedicated path: wipe, pre-move wall, avatar-only contact (armed or not), armed projectile interception, double scatter movement, then proximity arming or existing-armed homing. Contact uses the stored velocity, even while stationary. The arming Run does not home; an already armed mine can scatter and home in one Run. The empty enemy-array guard is retained. Pcode `_‑XK:399` resolves the malformed no-contact loop to false. Successful projectile contact calls1834 first, then the incoming projectile's callback; an ordinary incoming shell survives.
- `AssetPack.projectileBounds` supplies field-coordinate Script bounds. Transforms compose down to each declared leaf before union; transparent children and masks contribute without clipping or filter growth. Dynamic beam line/mask widths and endpoints, turret construction/owner-directed rotation, projectile scale and root rotation are included. Morphs use declared start bounds. This follows [Ruffle0.7.1 bounds and hitTestObject](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/display_object.rs#L1312) and its [morph Script branch](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/display_object/morph_shape.rs#L114). Edge/corner inclusion and invalid empty bounds were checked against the [rectangle comparator on master](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/master/swf/src/types/rectangle.rs#L140); that comparator was not fetched from the pinned tag. An empty authored frame has zero width and no contact geometry, rather than falling back to a mock circle. Mock mode uses its drawn bounds. These are emulator implementation references, not an Adobe Flash runtime comparison.
- `tools/import-mine-art.ts` recovers only numerical IDs/counts/labels from the local SWF. Seeker2178 arms at41, frame40 jumps to1 and rerolls a rounded0…10 start, and55 stops without expiring or stopping homing. The root MovieClip clock continues during gameplay pause/collapse/crossing. Ordinary enemy1792 and ally2182 inherit `_‑BL.frame1` random starts and loop16→2. Ally2182 removes every child at7, intentionally blinking until the loop returns to2. No artwork is exported. Exact Flash behavior for randomized frame0, independently randomized child clocks and stage event interleaving remains unverified.
- Ordinary `_‑U9`/`_‑M1` mines stay stationary and have no wall-expiry branch; their source speed10 only supplies the inherited backward-half-step contact vector. Ally fire now selects its own2182 artwork. - Whip3014 (`_‑Rx:59–118`, pcode329–407) polls exact root frame31 before contact/follow. It checks its previous location before following the retained emitter, keeps launch rotation and survives owner jam. Pausing can pass31 and wrap through the random frame1 callback, so there is no invented30-active-frame expiry. A detached owner retains the last safe position until authored expiry rather than deliberately throwing on a stale source reference; this bounded guard is explicit.

Seekers/orbitals retain separate multi-bubble layouts rather than another generic dot.

Masks use their recovered rectangular extents; Flak uses paired cells and bubbles have contained procedural highlights/shading. Strokes sit inside the declared footprint. Per-polygon bounds provide a cheap rejection before point tests. The prior radius fallback remains only for unknown future symbols. The native SWF rendering and alpha-mask contact path is unchanged.
- `mock-part.ts` uses translucent blue body film, pale rims and larger irregular glints. Player weapons use warm yellow, utility parts gold, and enemy weapons amber/orange as requested. Body interiors retain alpha above the existing mask threshold, so the visual change does not turn their contact into hollow rings. Mock mode remains a procedural approximation: original contour paths, gradients, filters and antialiasing still require the player's SWF.
- Thirteen focused geometry/contact tests cover scaled visible diameters, source palettes, distinct seeker/orbital clusters, rotation, retained child poses, empty frames, morph ratios, dynamic masks and shoot-down outside the former radius. An additional test independently reads the original local SWF and compares every authored frame of all34 imported roots with numeric bounds. The browser comparison renders original and mock weapons and nine projectile states at the same scale, checks matching Script dimensions, palette/body opacity, painted panel pixels and final canvas hash. It proves actual enemy-seeker interception with both supplied-SWF and mock collision adapters while leaving the incoming shot alive.

- `_‑14/_‑c4.TestForCollision:40–68` (pcode243–274) chooses the first enemy clip in the live bullet array with strict distance `<11.5+Script width/2`, without checking enemy-tank count, prior state2, or queued deletion. - `BTAGameWorld.Run:489–541` changes gameplay polling in pause/transit without recursively stopping tank children. `_‑Rm:187–194` removes background ENTER_FRAME listeners only. ### Global ink and collapse screen effects

- `_‑ar.BulletCallCollisionDetected:15–24` and matching pcode64–116 always queue impact, then test the target's existing ink boolean. `InkMe(120)` (`_‑Zx:134–140`) changes only avatar-design tanks. - `_‑2c.CreateEffect:65–82` preserves registration/scale and applies rounded random0…360 rotation when requested. ### Main-level shake, flashes and the shared stage clock

- `_‑1w.as:27–38,123–161,217–249` registers a separate Main `ENTER_FRAME` listener before `Main.Run` (`Main.as:269,372`). Its shake and flash clocks run through pause, collapse, crossings and menus. `MainEffects` and the browser `StageRuntime` now preserve that ownership and ordering. The browser advances effects once before the viewport's gameplay/input step; headless `World.step` advances its own controller by default. Wall Timer callbacks still receive actual elapsed time, while frame catch-up retains the existing100ms bound.
- `SHAKE(N)` only accepts a request when enabled and the prior duration is exactly zero. It decrements duration before processing, samples every third stage frame and holds the resulting displacement between samples. Original duration greater than45 selects amplitude4; other requests use2. Each axis uses the source biased rounded random roll, scaled by remaining/original duration. Requests of1–3 frames never move Main. The source's double addition is intentional here: `_‑D9` assigns the candidate, then `_‑bS` adds rounded(candidate−oldPosition), confirmed in pcode1040–1210 and403–473. Reset passes the baseline itself to `_‑bS` (pcode578–583), including its nonzero-baseline defect. Disabling while already at baseline leaves an active duration intact, as the source does.
- Main position setters truncate to twentieth-pixel twips after each assignment; alpha setters truncate to8.8 fixed point. This follows the reference runtime's [DisplayObject wrappers](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/avm2/globals/flash/display/display_object.rs#L314), [Twips conversion](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/swf/src/types/twips.rs#L102), [alpha property](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/display_object.rs#L654), and [Fixed8 conversion](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/swf/src/types/fixed.rs#L73). For example, a7-frame shake with roll1 first stores0.55 then adds1, producing1.55; flash alpha is243/256 after one update and9/256 after19. The negative twentieth update remains tracked until removal21. These numerical contracts match Ruffle0.7.1; controlled Adobe Flash comparison remains open.
- Each `FLASH(color, fillAlpha)` owns a separate rectangle at−40,−40. This compositor currently uses the logical550×450 viewport plus an80px margin. Fill alpha remains independent of clip alpha, which starts1, subtracts0.05 through the display-property setter each stage frame and is removed on the following update after reaching zero. Overlaps composite in creation order. The source `_‑UT` only detaches array index0, but normal chronological flashes expire in that same order; no base caller changes individual clip alpha or invokes `_‑UT` directly. This implementation tracks the observable chronological lifecycle without retaining unreachable transparent children.
- `MainStage` translates the whole logical550×450 surface, including canvas, HUD and DOM menus. A separate transparent canvas composites flash rectangles above the game interface. Standalone fixture renderers apply the same controller to their complete Pixi stage; production uses the DOM transform/compositor to avoid applying the displacement twice. Resize/fullscreen scale remains outside Main. Transform updates precede pointer-coordinate reads, so a stationary pointer keeps its correct local coordinates while Main moves. Idle transforms and empty flash canvases avoid repeated DOM writes and clears.
- Base callers are now source-backed: new-game FLASH after graphics setup (`Main.as:1112–1119`); Massive Cannon3008 completion conditionally SHAKE20, then FLASH, then spawn (`_‑ct/_‑YM.as:25–35`); teleport3011/3012 FLASH between effect registration and relocation (`_‑1W/_‑QQ:50–54`); Apocalypse's increasing shake requests and independently spaced flashes (`_‑0D:196–219`). Ordinary `_‑Zx.Damage` and `PlaceAvatar` do not request either effect, so the invented damage shake and replacement shake/flash were removed. Other shipped effect callers have no base-game constructor path and remain outside the active catalog.
- Collapse state3 rejects pausing, matching `BTAGameWorld.PauseMenuSetup:1141`; the independent shrink listener is not frozen behind a pause panel. Normal travel remains pausable as the existing requested usability extension.
- Settings retain the source asymmetry: main-menu shake controls and both efficiency controls call controller enable/disable and update the saved flag; pause's standalone shake control only updates the flag, gating Massive Cannon's explicit check. Loading the saved flag does not itself disable the controller. FLASH has no disable setting. This follows `_‑Sm:823–831,1146–1182`, `PauseMenu:540–546,1184–1208` and `Main.as:734`.
- New deterministic checks cover held samples, random draw count, amplitude threshold, request rejection, disable/reset quirks, nonzero baseline, separate flash fill/fade/lifetimes, stage-before-game order, no duplicate advancement, viewport-unmounted effects, FPS/catch-up, paused world states, Massive callback order and collapse completion retention. Browser checks mount the production MainStage and GameViewport in both asset modes, checking whole-surface geometry, stationary-pointer aim, flash alpha compositing, continued menu effects and35/90FPS changes. Existing production beam release and wall-timed rocket checks cover the clock refactor.
- A remaining geometry difference is explicit: source `stageRef.width/height` queries display-list Script bounds, not stageWidth/stageHeight. [Stage accessors](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/avm2/globals/flash/display/Stage.as#L197) delegate to the [recursive Script-bound union](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/display_object.rs#L1409). Retained flash rectangles and offstage game content can therefore enlarge subsequent rectangles beyond630×530. The current rectangle still covers the visible viewport under ordinary base-game shakes, but exact offstage extent has not been recreated or verified. A complete Main display-list geometry provider is needed before claiming this metadata matches Flash.
- Evidence boundary: these are script/pcode contracts and checks of the reconstruction. Synchronized Flash recordings, Adobe runtime comparison of the recovered transform/alpha quantization, absolute constructor/event-loop interleaving, complete collapse choreography and broader screen/creator transitions still require comparison. The complete one-to-one goal remains active.

See the [kernel calculation](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/src/filters/blur.rs#L195-L270), [blur shader](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/shaders/filter/blur.wgsl#L43-L72), [knockout composition](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/shaders/filter/glow.wgsl#L39-L75), and [filter padding/scaling](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/swf/src/types/blur_filter.rs#L34-L49). Its10px padding/kernel stay in logical stage coordinates, independent of field/child transforms. General GlowFilter families, other Flash filters, exact Stage/DPR filter scaling and Adobe antialiasing remain unsupported or unverified. The source filter's knockout flag is essential: erasing the original solid shape would produce a different effect.
- New rule/parser tests cover captured registration/orientation, birth continuation, retained frame1,119 shrink steps, callback/removal order, separate field transforms and filter flags. Native/mock browser galleries exercise the fixed camera, avatar/shot/field shrink, squared bottom effects, completion flash, isolated ERASE and the nested inherited-ERASE/glow path. ### Filter arithmetic preparation

- `assets/glow-kernel.ts` separates the recovered fractional box kernel and repeated-pass padding table from WebGL rendering. Kernels retain the 255-pixel cap, skipped axes at width≤1 and quantized fractional edge weights. Padding retains the reference emulator's approximate pass-growth factors; it is not a claim of exact Adobe filter bounds.
- `assets/swf-color-transform.ts` models parent-after-child transforms with signed Fixed8 multiplier wrapping and signed 16-bit offsets. Four focused tests cover fractional/disabled kernels, pass growth, twip truncation, negative multipliers and overflow.
- The arithmetic portion of the renderer checkpoint was landed separately for review. The following checkpoint integrates these helpers into live painting. All 386 current rule/parser tests and 5200 assertions pass with the local research files available. The full recreation goal remains incomplete.

### Native glow stacks and post-filter color transforms

- The original binary contains193 GlowFilter records across34 sprite definitions, with65 unique descriptors: blur0…26, strength0…4, one to three passes, inner/outer and knockout combinations, varying RGBA, and stacks of up to two filters. No authored part-art root among the93 catalog parts contains a glow. Active retained artwork includes the fire HUD1506, minimap1520, large life bubble1820, Apocalypse2058, armed seeker mine2178 and collapse2173. The renderer now consumes every authored glow descriptor in order, replacing the former collapse-only predicate. Animated parameters update existing filter uniforms rather than recompiling shaders or replacing filter objects.
- `SwfGlowFilter` implements the pinned WGPU [glow branches](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/shaders/filter/glow.wgsl#L39-L75) and [fractional blur passes](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/src/filters/blur.rs#L183-L270). Horizontal then vertical repeats for each pass; each executed axis floors to8bit, widths cap at255, and an axis at width≤1 is skipped. A zero-pass/no-axis filter still composes against the original alpha. Strength saturates before the glow-color alpha is applied; knockout takes priority over composite-source. Padding follows the reference's pass-growth table and twip truncation. Ruffle explicitly calls this multi-pass bounds table approximate relative to Flash. Kernels remain in logical stage pixels; exact Stage/DPR scaling, pixel snapping and Adobe rasterization remain comparison gaps.
- Filtered objects capture their children with identity accumulated color, then apply their own and ancestor placement transforms to the filtered bitmap, as in [render_base's capture/final draw](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/core/src/display_object.rs#L1077-L1129). Unfiltered native branches now pass accumulated color to their leaves; a filter boundary resets that inheritance for its capture. This prevents faded knockout artwork from developing an interior ghost and applies2178's multiply/add tint to the final orange halo. Native tank/creator embedding opacity, protection/status decorations, projectile alpha and HUD opacity use explicit view inputs rather than attenuating filter capture through Pixi ancestors. Fire HUD's previously omitted202/256 top-level opacity is now applied.
- Transform composition follows [signed Fixed8 wrapping multiplication and signed16-bit additive terms](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/swf/src/types/color_transform.rs#L88-L116). The custom bitmap color shader unpremultiplies, transforms, saturates and premultiplies in the [default bitmap-shader order](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/v0.7.1/render/wgpu/shaders/bitmap.wgsl#L35-L51), preserving fully transparent pixels. Simple unfiltered alpha-only leaves retain Pixi's packed-alpha path; its byte quantization is not an Adobe pixel-parity claim. Existing collision providers and Script bounds remain separate from filter-expanded visual coverage.
- Authored cacheAsBitmap flags now establish the corresponding isolated bitmap/color boundary. Ordinary LAYER isolation does not itself move the accumulated color after the whole group: its children still receive that color individually. A focused overlapping-child check distinguishes their192 versus128 expected alpha under a half-opacity owner. This models the compositing boundary without retaining a bitmap between draws; full Flash cache invalidation/reuse and cached-bitmap pixel snapping remain open.
- The development-only oracle independently composes byte images for all65 original parameter sets, the actual two-filter HUD stack, and13 controls covering every glow branch, zero passes/axes, inherited fade, post-glow tint, saturation and additive alpha. Browser comparisons read raw GPU premultiplied bytes directly. Pixi8.22's WebGL canvas extraction places those bytes into Canvas2D without unpremultiplying them; the shared fixture capture helper now converts extracted galleries to straight RGBA to avoid darkening the evidence twice. Existing galleries that read the live application canvas already receive the browser's normal conversion.
- Remaining scope is explicit: static SVG-composed thumbnails/menu/win artwork still needs equivalent authored-filter rendering; seven ActionScript-created glow call sites, including hover/focus effects, require their separate runtime integration. Other Flash filter families, general cache invalidation and controlled original-runtime recordings remain unfinished. These source-contract checks improve the retained native artwork; they do not establish complete Flash or game parity.
- Incremental landing verification: all 386 rule/parser tests with 5200 assertions, TypeScript, formatting and a production build pass. The complete current browser suite passes 63 cases in mock/supplied-SWF modes; only the optional original-player smoke is skipped (4.9 minutes). Both glow cases pass, including all 65 authored variants, the two-filter HUD stack and synthetic/cache controls. The native life-bubble/mine/HUD/collapse gallery was visually inspected. CI now includes the synthetic GPU comparison without requiring original artwork. This is the runtime integration following the separately landed arithmetic; it does not establish full-game or Adobe parity.

### Transform-dependent geometric stroke width

- Contact now carries the full inverse linear matrix from logical Stage coordinates to each leaf. With inverse columns `(a,b)` and `(c,d)`, local full width is `max(authoredWidth, hypot(a,b), hypot(c,d))`, following [the pinned Ruffle calculation](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac/render/src/shape_utils.rs#L789-L797). Each descent composes `inverse(child-to-parent) × parentInverse`; nested unequal scaling and rotation must not multiply scalar maxima. Geometry queries use round caps/joins, matching the reference segment-distance implementation rather than authored paint flags. Declared shape bounds still reject outside points and are not expanded to this minimum.
- Retained clips, stateless symbol traversal, masks, mounted parts, native status/protection attachments, projectiles and creator selection carry that matrix. GameViewport supplies game zoom to the contact providers; browser CSS enlargement and rendering resolution/DPR do not enter the calculation. Collapse skips gameplay contact in `World.stepFrame`, and transit translation does not change the linear matrix. Procedural fitted silhouettes remain approximate.
- `python3 tools/reference/hit-test-probe.py /tmp/bt3-probe-thin.swf --thin-strokes` reproduces the 1220-byte payload and SHA256 recorded above. Without the flag, the original 970-byte payload/hash is unchanged. The saved controlled replay is reused because the generated input bytes are identical. It corroborates positive thin strokes at scale 1 and 0.5 and authored-bounds rejection for dynamic Sprite graphics; static/morph application is source-backed. Adobe behavior, exact twip rounding, non-scaling stroke flags and broader display-tree/event parity remain unverified.
- The browser regression first failed four expected hits (full scale, half scale, nested anisotropic and reflected). The corrected provider passes those cases, enlarged/authored-width limits, singular transforms, round endpoint distance and bounds rejection, plus scaled/rotated tank parts, game zoom, creator geometry and both retained/stateless projectile paths. A rule test independently checks the inverse of a nested unequal-scale/rotation product. Full-game parity remains open.

The fixture now uses a fresh, disposed pack. A fresh build and all six focused contact/native creator cases then pass, covering both contact modes, native selection/group editing, placement, class measurement and saved copies. No production cache workaround was added. The passing broad flows cover mock/synthetic gameplay and affected native damage, protection, projectiles and retained animation. Exact boundary/twip and complete parity limits above remain.


### Independent mounted-shield artwork and callbacks


- `AGunShield.PartCallTrigger:27–54` keeps its film (`_-2j`) and first-use size cache (`_-IP`) on each weapon instance. `_-Zx.RegisterSecondaryWeapon:692–696` registers every mount without deduplication. Each mount therefore creates its own retained hit clip once and a fresh film on every trigger. First use overwrites only the tank's `movShieldHit` pointer; retriggering an earlier mount does not redirect hits back to it.
- `shields.ts` now records each mounted hit/film separately, including cached diameter, stage age, impact rotation and direct-child identity. Native rendering and geometric contact use these records; the mock fallback paints separate rings in the same child order. Earlier hit instances remain attached and keep finishing independently. Removed films release their renderer instances; other mounts' films retain theirs.
- Every trigger queues its own completion callback bound to that mount. `KillShield:57–62` removes the mount's **current** film and clears the shared immunity flag, even while another mount's film remains attached. An older callback can therefore remove a newer retriggered film on that same mount. The implementation preserves that ownership rather than cancelling older effects or capturing the original film. Normal secondary cooldown is60 frames and completion is25; deliberately forced repeated triggers exercise the otherwise inaccessible overlap. The JavaScript cleanup tolerates an already missing film rather than reproducing Flash's invalid-removeChild exception.
- Arrival still clears immunity and queued effects without detaching films. All surviving films keep independent stage clocks through arrival and pause. Damage starts/rotates only the hit clip last assigned by a first-use trigger; an in-progress impact does not restart until its terminal frame. The26-frame film and25-frame effect clocks remain separate.
- Coverage includes staggered mounts with different cached sizes, independent completion and shared immunity, retained old hits, latest-pointer impact routing, same-mount retrigger callback ownership, and multiple retained arrival films. The native/mock browser gallery checks actual rendered child identities, removal/reuse, independent clocks/sizes and geometry contributed by the larger film. Older turret hit instances, complete live-child sizing and synchronized original-runtime comparison remain open; full game parity remains unproven.

### Turret protection ownership and retained impact clips


- `_-ct/_-DO.BehaviorRun:25–65` scans that launcher's own `_‑BC` references, pruning detached turrets and enabling the shared shield flag when any retained turret's `inner_mc` reaches4. Its `Run:90–99` polls this before aiming and firing. The previous reconstruction refreshed a two-frame shield timer from each mature projectile's Run, which enabled protection in the wrong phase and let it expire while jam/stun prevented the owner weapon from updating.
- Each gun now retains its own turret references. Protection polls at the owner weapon's source phase; construction completed by a later builder therefore becomes protective on the next owner Run. Jam, stun and pause keep the existing immunity latched until polling resumes. Losing one of several mature turrets preserves the film; losing the last one removes the mount's film and clears the current hit pointer.
- Source cleanup removes only the film and **does not remove the old hit display child**. Rebuilding creates another hit/film pair at the cached tank-radius diameter while earlier hits keep their own age/rotation and finish independently. These identities now participate in native display order and geometric contact. `_-KJ.frame16` and `_-bM.frame25` stop the hit and film at their terminal frames; source style and frame selection are shared by native painting and contact. Mock rings follow the same lifetime/order, with their existing approximate paint.
- Launcher aim now tracks its incomplete turret every active frame, then returns toward the field origin after completion/detachment (`EnemyAim:101–142`). `PartCallTrigger:73–87` explicitly passes the hostile flag for both turret and builder, including an allied custom design; that source quirk is retained. The original global random stream is still not reproduced.
- Rules cover real builder completion/update order, jam/stun latching, last-turret cleanup, reconstruction with retained older hits, latest-pointer impact routing, stopped artwork clocks through pause, construction-target aim, and the explicit hostile flag. Source multi-launcher ordering shares one immunity flag and pointer; cleanup tolerates an absent own film rather than reproducing invalid `removeChild(null)` exceptions. Controlled original runtime comparisons and full live-child protection sizing remain open. Full game parity remains unproven.

### Hidden attached parts in the headless fallback

Its circles remain approximate; this does not establish contour or complete collision parity. The browser installs its own retained geometry callbacks.

The preceding fallback passes the visible assertion and fails the hidden assertion. Formatting, TypeScript and all 427 rule/parser tests with 5604 assertions pass after the correction.
