### Complete progression using ordinary control frames

- `tools/playthrough/pilot.ts` is a development-only observer/controller. It starts a fresh `World([], [],17)` with the original starter, level 0 and 1 health, then issues movement, aim, primary/secondary input, offered-upgrade selection and Close for secondary help. It never sets health, progression, enemies, pickups, fields, bullet damage or random state. It waits for field completion and follows real boundary crossings to explore. The production game receives no pilot route or fast-forward feature.
- `tools/playthrough/browser.ts` installs the browser asset collision/bounds/protection callbacks at default game zoom 1, advances complete simulation frames, draws milestones with the production `GameRenderer`, and mounts the actual `WinPanel` after `World` reaches `won`. The browser gate checks all six offered upgrades, normal drops, boss generation/defeat, the Boss Badge, win preparation/fade and the WinPanel Continue event. Evidence includes milestones, stats and an input fingerprint.
- The pilot stays within unfinished fields to preserve the source's win-suppression quirk during transit. In `re/bt3/export_scripts_deobf/scripts/BTAGameWorld.as:1446–1466`, `PreWinScreenSetup` rejects states 1/2/3, including transit; `CompletedABubblefield:781–797` still awards the badge. No game balance or completion logic changes in this split. The headless hidden-part correction was landed separately above.
- Scope: accelerated automated control-frame sessions read world state and use the offered-upgrade API. They do not drive every menu with native keyboard/pointer events, render every simulation frame, reproduce the original global RNG, compare a complete Adobe/Ruffle playthrough, or prove human difficulty/balance or pixel/audio timing parity. The Continue event check does not exercise continued-world persistence. Complete game parity remains unproven. Repeat with `bun run test:e2e e2e/playthrough.spec.ts`, or select “Complete control-only playthrough” in `bun run fixtures` and provide a local SWF/use mock assets. No SWF or captured original artwork is committed.
- Verification: formatting, TypeScript, all 427 rule/parser tests with 5604 assertions and the production build pass. Both full runs and all four native/mock body-damage/contact cases pass. Mock wins in 80,698 frames with 1252 kills and 194 visited fields; supplied SWF wins in 75,904 frames with 1185 kills and 184 visited fields. Both take all six offered upgrades with zero downgrades. A second native run reproduces the complete JSON, including input fingerprint 3506681754. All 48 mock/synthetic cases pass in the full suite; its mock full run also reproduces the first JSON. Both proportioned milestone sheets and win panels were visually inspected. Raw metadata, logs and captures remain local under ignored `reference-captures/playthrough/`. These results establish accelerated progression for this pilot/seed and asset modes; complete original-game parity remains open.

### Flash logical keys and input latches

- `_‑Ay.as:44–188,205–212` captures received numeric key codes without rejecting duplicates. `hotkeys.ts` maps supported browser codes to that logical domain, preserves the source punctuation/numpad labels and its unretained `toUpperCase()` return: F1 displays lowercase `p`. - `AvatarInterface.as:519–533,558–567,675–727` keeps unique logical key codes and removes a code on keyup. Its Up/else-Down and independent Left/else-Right chains give Up/Left priority for same-axis duplicate bindings while permitting a shared key across axes. Space assigned horizontally cannot start secondary fire; vertical Space still can. If secondary was already latched, changing Space to horizontal while held leaves it latched until release. The browser controller now preserves those contracts and suppresses browser defaults for bound keys.
- Source lines729–733 sample the lock key once per frame with `blnHoldHot6`. The backlog’s event-based callback incorrectly fired twice when two physical modifier keys mapped to one logical code. `GameInput.frame()` now owns the held-key edge; a key pressed and released between sampled frames triggers no lock callback. Dialog shortcut handling uses the same canonical code, preserving layout-effect focus transfer and permitting either modifier alias to resume a paused game. The base offline game still has no lock registration target.

Existing preferred-FPS/session semantics and zoom-aware geometric hit callbacks remain intact.

### Native menu geometry API (pointer wiring pending)

- `MainMenuView.hit` now queries the current retained title, series and control poses. Imported depths place title545 above series524 above the controls; named series children are checked before their background. Interactive inner490 is checked before static icon decoration, matching [Ruffle's interactive-child priority](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac/core/src/display_object/movie_clip.rs#L3170-L3207). Source `mmBtnUp` branches on `target.parent.name`, so a wrapper-only icon hit returns decoration rather than an action. The specialized API does not model retired login555 or the rollover TextField504 as interactive targets.
- Point conversion alone loses the ancestors needed for stroke contact. The API now composes the complete inverse for title, series, wrappers and directly queried inner/series children before calling `AssetPack.hitClip`. [Ruffle's graphic query](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac/core/src/display_object/graphic.rs#L280-L294) supplies that inverse; its [stroke minimum](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac/render/src/shape_utils.rs#L642-L648) depends on inverse-column lengths. In particular, Play's3.399993896484375 scale requires a minimum about0.294118 local pixels, rather than1 local pixel. Its transparent zero-width morph489 contour exists during hover frames2–6; frame7 replaces it with unstroked488. A native regression finds a real in-flight contour point at(207.85,213.674929...) and fails with the backlog picker restored, then passes with the full inverse. This is a backend/retained-art integration check, not a fresh Adobe boundary observation.
- Distant static leaves now reject the same declared bounds already checked by `ShapeContact.hit` before allocating paths. The headless regression verifies false contact and zero new cached shapes. Morph interpolation and the shared fill/stroke predicate remain unchanged; the generic inverse helper's existing epsilon is not broadened in this slice, and inspected menu placements are nondegenerate.
- A development-only gallery drives hover poses from the independently recorded source targets, then queries the production view. It matches all12 recorded target rows, rejects action hits in every button corner, checks the capsule and four links across all19 series frames, and exercises the transient Play stroke. Reachable title545 contains four sprites and17 shape leaves, with no text/bitmap/morph leaves; its queries therefore use geometric shape contact rather than text raster fallback. The gallery renders supplied art and releases its Pixi resources on removal. No source media or generated data changes are committed.

### Menu pointer dispatch and accessible input

- `MenuPointer` now owns stage-wide physical hover and captured left `mouseup` dispatch. Client coordinates map through the current CSS rectangle to the original550×450 stage, independent of device pixels. A release uses its current target without requiring a press on that control, matching `_‑Sm.mmBtnUp` and the instrumented original's outside-press/Settings-release observation. DOM mouse clicks do not dispatch a second action; detail-zero keyboard clicks retain the accessible route.
- Native input queries `MainMenuView.hit` after updating retained poses, including the full inverse stroke correction above. A changed target updates hover commands before painting. Mock input uses its procedural ellipses/capsule. Every draw refreshes picking even without pointer motion, so changed art and series link lifetimes can change the target. Wrapper-only decoration retains a hand cursor without activating its inner action; title geometry blocks underlying controls during the intro.
- Real pointer movement takes ownership from keyboard hover. A later blur of the formerly focused button clears keyboard state while preserving physical coordinates. Tab focus still drives the retained hover and series reveal; both modes retain accessible button names and offline link feedback. Handlers and covered-state refs update in the layout phase, and input listeners are removed with the retained renderer on cleanup, including StrictMode and initialization errors.
- Caption changes now share the rollover field rather than separate small DOM orb labels. Registered release targets clear it before the busy guard, including decorations and generic series background; title blockers do not run a release handler. Plain-text tracking, opaque white, per-leaf mock parent alpha and series bubbling/reversal remain as recovered in prior slices. My tanks/Change assets and unavailable website feedback remain deliberate offline replacements. The Play hint is currently fixed to “Play / Tutorial”; the original conditional tutorial preference is not yet represented by this menu's profile contract.
- The original pointer fixture's target-bearing rows are exercised through production input, alongside six empty control corners, icon-only release, hover-grown native Settings geometry outside its DOM rectangle, scaled-stage outside-press releases, right-button rejection, blur after physical movement and exactly-once keyboard/mouse actions. The intro fixture verifies that a visible Settings control cannot receive an action through title art. Ordinary navigation now waits for native title frame179; intermediate input remains covered by controlled fixtures.
- Initial browser verification passed both intro and both pointer cases. Both series cases reached their final keyboard step, then failed because programmatic focus after pointer use did not establish `:focus-visible`. The tests now reach the strip with actual Tab input, preserving their frame/reversal/offline-link assertions. Formatting, TypeScript and475 rule/parser tests with5992 assertions pass. The final broader run passes all70 cases (8.2 minutes), including14 native cases and56 mock/synthetic cases; no selected cases skip. Native menu, Settings, Credits, series, FPS preference lifetimes, compressed import, creator/library round-trip, heavy tanks, existing pause/focus checks and the new pointer paths pass. These observations do not establish exact Adobe event delivery, every hit boundary, global constructor/frame phase or complete-menu/game parity.


### SWF button display-state metadata (creator wiring pending)

The existing action-presence flag remains metadata; no ActionScript executes. Unsupported filter records are consumed by the existing reader, rather than being presented as supported paint. The field order follows [Ruffle's pinned button reader](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle/blob/89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac/swf/src/read.rs#L797-L832); matrix and color readers already finish on byte boundaries, so their redundant alignment call and the unused filter skipper are removed.

### Creator selection-control rules (screen wiring pending)

- `nudgeSelection` applies a requested X step to selected parts without changing Y, twin links or unselected parts. Original `MoveRight`/`MoveLeft` and `MoveSelectionRight`/`MoveSelectionLeft` each apply one unit; the caller must retain both held per-frame updates and the separate click step rather than scale them by elapsed seconds. Input scheduling, class updates and history remain outside this pure helper.
- `centerSelection` follows `_‑F1` and `DeleteNonDominantTwinObject`: set the current selected part's Y to zero, remove a selected non-dominant twin, clear the retained owner's twin link and preserve unselected linked twins. It retains the source's indexed walk over a spliced selection. Given a faded-first ordering, removing the current slot can skip centering the shifted opaque owner. AS and pcode confirm the splice and increment; this is a bounded state/algorithm check, not an original-runtime observation that ordinary input reaches that ordering. A stable-iteration negative control fails that regression while the recovered index walk passes.
- `selectedTransform` averages scale and rotation across all selected parts, including faded twins, with empty defaults1/0. Rotations first map to the source slider's positive-turn representation; [DisplayObject's documented angle wrapping](https://airsdk.dev/reference/actionscript/3.0/flash/display/DisplayObject.html#rotation) explains normalization of serialized angles outside one turn before averaging. This is an arithmetic mean, so45° and−45° initialize at180°, not zero.
- Formatting, TypeScript,486 rule/parser tests with6040 assertions and production build pass. Four new rule cases cover centering/link preservation, X steps/ghost-inclusive means, the indexed splice case and empty/wrapped inputs. The controlled stable-walk run fails only the intended case (eight pass/one fail); the exact verified source was restored afterward. These helpers are not connected to production controls yet, so this slice changes no browser flow. Native/mock control wiring, held/release event order, actual slider gestures, runtime reachability and complete creator/game parity remain following work. Logs and the source audit stay ignored under `reference-captures/creator-selection-controls/`.

### Phasing constructor and active behavior clock

- The Phasing portion of original `928a5c6` replaces the `(actor age + UID phase) % 300` steering-time approximation. Source `_‑ct._‑Ai` starts an integer counter from a 0–50 roll; each active BehaviorRun increments it, subtracts0.02 while counter ≤ 50 and alpha > 0, adds0.02 while 150 < counter ≤ 200 and alpha < 1, and resets only the counter at 300. An initial counter 30 therefore has a partial first fade, rather than forcing complete invisibility. Boss movement no longer bypasses its registered behavior clock.
- The clock uses the existing, previously probed reference DisplayObject alpha setter. Per-assignment signed8.8 truncation corrects the old checkpoint's decimal accumulator: from counter 0/alpha 1, frame 20 stores136/256, frame 50 stores−1/256, frame 200 stores249/256, and frame 300 retains249/256 while resetting the counter. The negative residue remains in the model and paints transparent. This numeric adapter follows the reference runtime; Adobe equality remains unproven.
- `RegisterBehaviorPart` retains the first occurrence of each behavior ID in authored order without removing any design/visual parts. The source factory constructs a clip before registration, so even a rejected duplicate Phasing constructor consumes its counter roll. World/player rebuild, wave, ally and enemy-spawner factory calls now supply shared initialization context. Constructor/Setup interleaving across all other parts and behavior target acquisition remain unfinished; isolated tools use the deterministic fallback and do not establish whole-game RNG equality.
- Phasing runs through the part-update gate after movement. Jam, stun and paused worlds suspend its private counter/alpha rather than letting actor age advance the fade. The existing status representation stores duration plus the reset frame; its release boundary is preserved. Seven focused unit cases cover jam/reset/stun/pause, partial first-cycle alpha, complete windows/reset residue, unique registration with unchanged visual records, independent constructor draws/integer coercion and boss behavior. The initial regression observed alpha 0.94 during a jammed actor frame where it should remain 1.

## Healer data deactivation and existing-beam lifetime

- `_‑Zx.Deactivate` runs primary final callbacks and part deactivation, clears `objData`, then invokes the field callback that queues enemy removal. The clip remains parented until the next field Run. `Tank.dataActive` represents cleared data independently of retained design metadata and parent records. The later crossing audit below corrects the initial retained-artwork inference: TankData.WipeTank removes all children from the live actor. Part target references clear at deactivation, while existing HealerBeam home/patient references remain independent. The avatar's lethal-life path retains its data.
- `EBehaviorHealer.PartCallRun` reads the held patient's data inside its protected region. Failure stops the beam, clears the patient and retries raw acquisition outside that region; a queued deactivated candidate can make that retry escape. Acquisition does not filter dead entries, and a held reference still returns before new data reads. Aim still precedes the protected read. Only the known source read error joins the existing World-listener boundary; arbitrary host collision errors remain visible.
- `_‑Zx.AddLife` writes its signed integer life before reading `objData.numLife`. A still-parented deactivated patient gains the integer life, then aborts before body restoration/degradation. HealerBeam's contact counter resets only after AddLife returns, so it stays10 after this failure. Later bullets/avatar work wait for another Run; retained art and other stage listeners continue. The following frame's immediate beam removal can skip the next old live-array bullet for that frame, preserving source splice/iteration order.
- Category6 Healers receive inherited part Deactivate without their FinalPartCall. `HealerBeam.TestForCollision` tests direct home/patient parentage, without owner death/jam filtering. Existing beams therefore keep contacting after a same-frame owner kill or jam, then remove synchronously on actual home/patient detachment. Cached field wrappers must not be replaced with blanket on-stage filters.
- Evidence: decompiled `_‑Zx.as`, `Weapon.as`, `_‑ct/EBehaviorHealer.as`, `_‑14/HealerBeam.as`, `_‑0D.as` and exported Healer protected ranges establish these call boundaries. On unchanged master, the initial seven-case lifecycle suite passed its healthy control and failed six contradictory cases. The final thirteen focused regressions cover crossing damage, catch-side retry, beam contact/deactivation, raw acquisition, integer overflow, primary final-callback ordering, allied boundary exit and arbitrary-error propagation.
- Validation: formatting, TypeScript,750 unit tests and7,473 assertions pass. Production build and all ten affected mock/supplied-SWF browser cases pass without skips: lifecycle, targeting, damage artwork/contact, crossing and projectile order. The new six-cell gallery uses actual shell dispatch and each AssetPack's hitTest, without collision/error injection. Both galleries were inspected; same-pose beam-omission controls confirm visible paint for healthy, killed and jammed owners. The killed-patient case records life1/counter10, later bullet/avatar age0, continued stage callback and subsequent detach/recovery.
- Limits: these are source/pcode-derived behavior contracts checked in the recreation, not a controlled original-runtime or Adobe differential. The crossing error regression uses a deterministic contact adapter; the separate browser gallery verifies native contact in a single field. Broader infection-spawn versus primary-final-callback ordering, complete allied boundary-deactivation callbacks, constructor/event timing and full ordinary-input gameplay parity remain unfinished. This batch does not establish full NPC lifecycle or full-game parity.

### Native audio sample windows, music requests and collision sound clocks

- The18 original DefineSound MP3 entries carry positive seek1673/1674 and declared sample counts at22050Hz. The parser retained those numbers, but AssetPack previously discarded their timing and played raw MP3. Chrome decodes sound561 to5184 frames/235.102ms; the source window is seek1674/count2304, or104.4898ms. Asset preparation now decodes at the tag rate, selects that window before channel interleaving and emits a local16-bit PCM WAV before the gate reports ready. ## Source music scene requests and completion callbacks

- Main._-18 requests a fresh menu Sound before constructing each new menu, including an exit from the creator. Its game constructor separately requests the game Sound after setup. These calls belong to scene construction, not arbitrary profile changes or every visit to a retained child view. The shell now issues an explicit request for a fresh menu/game, separately from music enablement. Returning from the nested viewer to the creator retains music; exiting the creator to a new menu requests the same track again. Rejected fade-time and muted requests remain discarded. Enabling music restores the remembered accepted track without inventing another scene request, including the original remembered-menu quirk after muted game entry. Starting muted without any accepted track does not manufacture one on unmute.
- A fresh unmodified original guest session entered the enemy creator, completed its guide and exited through the public unsaved-changes confirmation. Mixed host PCM shows the outgoing track fade to near zero and a fresh menu start. A two-second post-return interval identifies source phase2.037234 at correlation0.999999909; continuous playback from the pre-return interval would instead reach phase28.670522. This is a finite decoder/resampler alignment, not physical-speaker or whole-audio equality. Chrome156/Ruffle0.7.1 JavaScript/WASM bytes were independently observed and matched installed bytes; that is not build provenance, complete execution coverage or Adobe equivalence.
- MusicPlayer's completion callback starts its Sound again and writes its full configured volume, even if a transition remains active. The next stage frame resumes that transition's existing counter. Browser music now uses finite playback and a completion callback rather than native infinite looping. The HTML media element is reused as a transport carrier: completion resets its position and volume but preserves the accepted destination and fade counter. Muting, replacement and disposal prevent a queued old completion from reviving a stopped voice. Pointer/key gesture recovery still retries the current carrier.
- The two new browser regressions fail on the preceding production build: creator return creates no new transition, and native looping never calls the replay path. Unit coverage checks cold-muted startup, same-track scene requests, settings-only updates, completion during both fade directions, destination retention, and stale callbacks after replacement/mute/disposal. Browser observation distinguishes music from effects by the source initial volumes; existing sound assertions continue to require an actual nonmusic media element.
- A bounded reachability audit fully consumed both embedded ABC payloads:4 and5355 method bodies,1619 and269117 instructions. The private charging dispatcher has no encoded QName or method-pointer uses; the public charging methods have no encoded QName uses or literal-name pushes in those payloads. Shock classes2366/2401 have only script registration uses and direct placements in loading-container frame2; sound1/569/570 occur in audio-bank571 frames10/11/12, while its frame1 script jumps to the stopped end frame25. These are negative static/placement findings, not a proof against arbitrary reflection or dynamically composed names. No new base-game shock/charging callback is justified by those definitions alone. Retired part-pack/latch paths remain explicit exclusions or unresolved reachability; they are not evidence of an ordinary playable route.
- Validation: formatting, TypeScript,773 unit tests/7,589 assertions and production build pass. All nine affected browser cases pass without skips with the local SWF: source windows, retained/new menu navigation in both asset modes, real mock/native completion replay, muted game entry, extracted volleys, gesture recovery and revisiting the cleared origin. The native menu case waits for the actual prepared track to finish; completion is not injected.

Samples were appended durably. The final shutdown summary query failed because Playwright closed its owned browser first; no complete-capture zero-error claim is made. Retired ad/analytics/parts-pack requests were blocked by the reference viewer's local network policy. Exact media-end/AVM event interleaving, one-sample loop boundaries, general constructor timing, full native/original boss/win comparison and complete-game parity remain unproved.

## Creator event coordinates and independent saved/display positions

- The source's selection handlers read `MouseEvent.stageX/stageY`, convert through `_‑6H.globalToLocal`, and write each delivered delta through `clip.x/y` before saving the next cursor. Palette `_‑Zl/_‑8a` also reads stage-event Points through `movTank.globalToLocal`; `StopLibraryDrag` commits the last moved pose. The earlier eighth-pixel browser assertion did not establish that contract. Input now follows the float32 matrix and signed-twip conversions, including device-pixel rounding and the target/stage/reference round trips. Each display write truncates independently. Matrix Point products use nearest/ties-even instead. The applied CSS scale supplies the view matrix; a painted bounding box can differ by about0.00003px at1.3×.
- `select_mc` is creator969's depth123 sprite867, at3719/4634twips with unit scale, zero alpha and cached bitmap. Its child866 has bounds±3500twips. The reference sprites and tank share the rings registration. A pinned Ruffle source revision (`89a7049965f3090e706265670df309a5128233ac`, principally `core/src/display_object.rs`, `render/src/matrix.rs`, MouseEvent getters and `swf/src/types/twips.rs`) supplies the numeric semantic guide. The observed WASM imports offsetX/offsetY and DPR as f64; an integer JavaScript/WASM import was not the cause. Matching loaded bytes does not prove that revision built the observed runtime.
- The original exports demonstrate more than final quarter-pixel movement. At4×, positive and negative eighth-pixel gestures can respectively leave x−20 unchanged and move it to−20.25. At3×, three thirds move−20.25 to−19.2. Fractional placement exports include x−40.35/y±10.3 and, at1.3×, x−50/y±10. Fresh captures with Chrome157/Ruffle0.7.1 reproduced all15 earlier exports, with64 foreground captures and no observed page errors. Loaded JavaScript/WASM identities were independently observed. - Source `arrTankDetails` stores raw imported/saved coordinates separately from display clips. Loading and undo construct clips through position setters without rewriting raw metadata. Nudges update raw x and the clip getter independently, including per-frame Number addition. Drag release copies selected surviving clip positions back; twin membership/link changes occur during dragging. `EditorDisplayState` now owns that transient display state, with separate saved/display observations for browser verification. Drawing, alpha hit tests, marquee geometry, outlines and `_‑6k` radial checks use display getters. `UpdateClass` instead builds fresh measurement clips from saved metadata. Rotation/scale controls preserve saved positions, and rejected edits rebuild the accepted display state.

## Creator marquee release order and active editor input bindings

- A fresh public original capture now resolves the earlier marquee/control trial. `_‑QV` sets the marquee flag; `_‑bp` clears its graphics without clearing that flag. The earlier stage `_‑0m` listener runs again on subsequent mouse-ups, before the later `_‑Xf`/`_‑1N` stage listeners copy the current selection's rotation/scale. `_‑VV` clears the gesture flags on a workspace click. Source and exported bytecode agree on that ordering. The marquee Sprite can become the release target after drawing; that target/click explanation is a display/source inference, not an AVM event trace.
- Inside- and outside-workspace marquees followed by either slider clear selection on release. The held clips/knob change, but public Save/Copy retains rotations0 and scales1. A real workspace click restores successful saves: rotations are88.42105263157895/−88.42105263157896 at DPR1 and90/−90 at DPR2; maximum scale is2. These outcomes must not be replaced by copying the selection captured at pointer-down. Rectangle samples use clip positions with the separately saved radius/scale; release-point alpha hits are independent of the last drawn rectangle. After the graphics are cleared, no rectangle samples remain.
- The same source capture distinguishes target mouse-up, stage release and subsequent click. Marquee centering and layering clicks retain the original metadata/order because their selection clears first. Delete's target mouse-up still removes the selected pair before that stage callback. A normal selected-part drag followed by rotation saves successfully and retains the chosen knob pose. Palette dragging detaches the workspace listener in `StartLibraryDrag` and rebinds it through `_‑JQ` in `StopLibraryDrag`, preserving selection and clearing the marquee flag; the next layering click works. Scrollbar drag uses the same detach/rebind contract, established by source. Held nudge history is recorded at target release; its extra click pixel reads the surviving selection afterward, rather than advancing before the stage callback.
- An additional public original rotation→marquee→center→Undo→Save/Copy probe retains the positive rotated pair after Undo, with zero observed JS/AVM errors and no intermediate Save. The center click therefore records an accepted no-op snapshot even though stage release cleared selection. Source `_‑0h` hides the controls; its listener-removal block is gated by a Delete `CLICK` listener that this path never registers. Unmounting the recreation's selection controls at pointer-up lost that subsequent click and skipped the history entry. They now remain mounted while hidden, preserving the real browser click without timers or an invented pointer-up commit. The first Undo keeps the rotation and the second returns to the preceding snapshot.
- Two repeatable original `release` captures provide36 public export snapshots and176 foreground captures. Both retain zero observed JavaScript page errors and zero observed AVM event-dispatch errors before manifest creation. The capture now records those error classes separately: JavaScript `pageerror` alone does not reveal an AVM2 failure. Loaded Chrome157/Ruffle0.7.1 JavaScript/WASM identities are independently recorded. The SWF digest remains`f5827841c682f988f23dcf470ab4ab59c1ffc497acb4ca0f21077d03f942c153`. ## Ordinary supplied-art gameplay and the original transition comparison boundary

- A fresh supplied-art recreation session earned its first Insidious upgrade through ordinary mouse/keyboard input, actual offered cards and normal browser time. A finite cached/live console capture retains1058 such dispatch errors in1086 events over a retained21.2-second interval; the original onset is not established by that cache. Its JavaScript page-error list did not expose those AVM errors. Public map input still worked, but game pause did not; the failed runtime was suspended through the reference viewer and retained for diagnosis. It is not a healthy in-game pause, an original upgrade/win result or evidence of Adobe behavior.
- Source `_‑Zx.Damage` calls `_‑1E(true)` again for a lethal level-zero hit without an explicit transit guard. The preceding recreation kept life1 and suppressed that second jump. The source audit below addresses that contract difference through field and mode ownership; it does not establish the cause of the recorded original/Ruffle failure. These sessions have different random encounters, scouting durations, zoom and observer strategies. Synchronized progression, frame/audio comparison, ordinary native/original boss/win and the complete recreation goal remain unfinished.

## Crossing frames, fresh wrappers and empty deactivated actors

- [The source and bytecode audit](crossing-source-reference.md) separates logical map coordinates, current/latest combat wrappers, superseded attached wrappers, cached actor/pose records and actual parent/callback ownership. Attachment advances logical coordinates before arrival and always constructs a fresh wrapper, including cache hits. Repeated lethal escape can replace the next wrapper without removing its predecessor. The orphan retains its last stage-relative position and artwork while its explicit combat Run stops. The source cache snapshots raw actor references and pose scalars, including queued deaths, under the saved previous logical coordinates. Reattachment restores poses and callbacks without resurrecting cleared data or primary targets. An older array reference cannot reparent a detached actor.
- Run selects its branch once. An interface callback opening an upgrade or secondary-help dialog does not cancel the rest of that frame. Raw execution state and the shared saved state now remain separate from attached dialog views. Arrival may restore transit with no destination; secondary-help Close writes ordinary mode while an attachment remains pending. Known read/callback errors end only the World listener, skipping its hit-timer tail while other stage/artwork work continues. Stunned cached empty actors finish their retained branch/release frame before active movement reads cleared data. Arbitrary host errors still propagate. The existing browser mid-crossing pause adapter preserves the crossing's saved state across its own pause/resume.
- Ordinary boundary exits attach and enable the birth gate before caller-owned final callbacks and shooting suppression. Death and Apocalypse's direct `_‑1E` calls retain the preceding shooting gate and wall timers. A nested death during an ordinary exit therefore does not enable shooting. Timer attempts during a direct crossing are rejected by the birth gate without canceling the remaining timer events. Completed outgoing wrappers clear shots/pickups before detachment; uncleared detached wrappers retain their collections. Destination collections and the separate global-effect clearing contract remain intact. Map links appear at attachment, and the visit/Explorer counter follows cache misses, including conditional repeated attachment.
- **Correction to the earlier Healer data/paint inference:** `TankData._‑Tr(3)` makes the EnemyAI itself `movTank`; Deactivate calls WipeTank, which removes every child before callback removal of the outer actor. A parented dead patient therefore has no body paint for a beam to contact. Its life stays0, its contact counter stays10 and later bullets/avatar work continue. Direct AddLife still increments signed life before a cleared-data read fails, but that method contract does not prove beam contact on an empty display. An emptied home can still support an existing beam contacting a healthy patient until actual detachment. Enemy and ally queues both flush at the next field frame; the earlier end-of-frame ally removal was an approximation. Galleries now verify empty supplied/mock paint and retain the separate raw-acquisition error boundary.
- Eleven crossing regressions cover selected-frame continuation, same-frame arrival/menu retention, shared help state, nested logical attachment, fixed orphan pose/art clocks, frozen cached poses, next-field damage projection, raw final-frame deaths, callback/array aliases, old-parent removal failure, completed-wrapper cleanup and caller shooting gates. The initial three frame regressions and two ownership regressions fail on the preceding behavior. The new renderer proof keeps the abandoned body at the same stage position through camera motion, advances its artwork without actor Run and retains a positive same-pose body-omission paint assertion in both asset modes. The corrected Healer and NPC galleries were inspected alongside native/mock crossing captures.
- Validation: formatting, TypeScript and827 unit tests/7,745 assertions pass. Production builds and all30 distinct affected browser cases have passing runs without skips across mock and supplied-SWF modes, including crossing, Healer targeting/lifecycle, NPC teardown, damage, pickups, production input timers, pause/Info, forcefield lifecycle/order and local game/SWF sharing flows. After the final caller and ownership corrections, all16 crossing, forcefield, production-input and game/SWF cases pass on the final build. The new gallery's initial high-DPI canvas crop was corrected without reducing paint assertions. The compressed-SWF flow now installs the existing first-use guide handler before dragging; its timeout trace showed the guide intercepting input. The supplied and mock galleries retain their positive body/beam omission controls; source media and captures remain uncommitted.
- Limits: these are recovered base-game source contracts and recreation fixtures, not a synchronized original/Adobe differential. The retained original failure's actual null receiver and triggering second hit remain unknown; a separate continuous three-minute original run did not reproduce it. The after-death-explosion definition sets a different stopped state, but no enabling caller was found across the661 exported AS/pcode pairs; dynamic/external reachability remains unproved. Arbitrary nested callback combinations, exact detached-clip/constructor/RNG timing, whole-frame compositing, ordinary native/original boss/win and the complete recreation goal remain unfinished.


## Offline upgrade defaults and the live avatar library

- `UpgradeMenu.GetDefaultTank` searches cached profile records backwards, requiring exact class, exact current GP budget and exact current tier. The previous random equally priced pool could offer Insidious instead of the fresh source Infiltrator and ignored saved avatars unless they happened to share the enemy-roster argument. `core/upgrade-offers.ts` now applies the recovered predicate and preserves the most recent matching saved record. All31 builtin avatar codes match the source `_‑7G` array in order. Read [the source contract](upgrade-source-reference.md) for the complete six-level default table. The original level-two capture shows Nimble / Heavy Seeker / Punisher.
- Profile cache `TIER` uses the greater of the GP-threshold count and the zero-based `_‑JY` row returned by `_‑HM`; it is not the playable one-based part unlock level. Zero GP thresholds count.
